<retained-input>
# Purpose Context Product

## Product boundary

Purpose Context owns the purpose context index contract, canonical authoring
and consumption instructions, source paging/search, deterministic validation,
rule selection, Markdown rendering, artifact checks and reusable index catalog.
Its stable definition ID is `urn:stdo:product-definition:purpose-context`.

The runtime accepts an explicit source corpus, exact method identity, purpose
and query facts. It returns selected rules with conditions, exceptions,
dependencies, precedence, source grounds and unresolved decisions preserved.
A catalog index must match the caller's actual source membership/order/bytes,
method, purpose and family applicability. New source or purpose requires re-entry.

The corpus identity names the reusable source, not a downstream project.
Target Product, task and existing grant are caller coordinates. Multiple projects
may reuse the same authored index when the governing inputs actually match.

## Construction authority

The owner selected this Product and the extraction in the
[alignment activation](../.ai-workspace/work/ALIGNMENT_2026-11-12/ACTIVATION.json).
The Product Definition pins installed STDO 3.0.4 and the
[reference-frame basis](REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS.md). This constructor basis is
separate from each caller's source/method selection. No all-3.* compatibility
is implied by the initial catalog entry.

## Relations and exclusions

STDO Skills owns host adapters, discovery and exact dependency pins. It reads
this Product's one canonical skill and invokes its CLI. It does not own a
second engine. Specification Methodology owns the governing STDO sources and
native method toolchain. Representation/Axiom may support visual documentation;
neither is a Purpose Context runtime dependency.

This Product grants no task authority, solves no task, proves no code correct
and makes no release decision. Mechanical validation and content equality do
not prove semantic completeness or that a recipient actually received a body.
Assessment and native delivery evidence remain separately bound subjects.

## Constraint coverage and task verification

A purpose declares the predicate its context is sufficient to decide. The
`python-pure-refactor/2` successor evaluates retention of applicable governing
method constraints before construction. A complete conditional obligation to
establish an actual domain, authority premise, design relation or proof does
not assert that the task already established it. The consumer must acquire its
concrete Product inputs and perform those obligations before the dependent
action or claim. Missing governing meaning or unknown requested permission
premises remain context gaps; future unperformed verification has no result.

The successor retains conditional ODD/UX branches within the original family,
uses explicit requested-permission predicates and never makes false profile
facts a family prerequisite. The complete source selection, purpose and exact
query bind each external semantic assessment. Product-specific and separately
adopted law beyond that scope remains a declared authoritative task input.
Finite source assessment is externally challengeable, not universal completeness.

## Current standing

The development-source predecessor established the concrete initial
index is the selected P1 working basis, ported by envelope rebinding while
preserving all 68 rule objects, processing ledger and unresolved entries.
Its historical 12-source-discriminator audit and three checked task outcomes
are finite observations, not new subject coverage. New contexts stay
`covered: false` without an exact separately selected assessment; material
unknowns still block coverage. An existing authorized experimental use may
carry a trial binding without creating new approvals or self-certifying.

The coverage successor retains all 68 original rule lineages and adds 45
source-derived imported/profile rules over sixteen exact installed owners.
Its 51 gap dispositions distinguish imported law, actual applicability or
permission premises, and task parameters/future verification. The
[current coverage record](../skills/stdo-context/indexes/stdo-3.0.4-python-pure-refactor-v2/COVERAGE-STANDING.json)
binds the separately supplied supported assessment for exact candidate03/Q0
and the retained falsified candidate02. The unchanged authoring provenance is
historical. A new query receives no inherited verdict; this catalog or its
author never self-certifies it. Original P1 remains exact and its historical
evidence is not transferred.

Publication, public qualification and general effectiveness require their own exact evidence; none is inferred from these historical observations.

## Historical latest-method index target

The owner selected the latest releasable semantic source for release-target
index authoring, independently of this Product's older construction basis.
The separate `stdo-3.0.5-rc.1-python-pure-refactor-v2` entry binds the latest
selected published native cut and the same sixteen-owner closure. Its 113
rule meanings and family are conserved, with two RELEASE source coordinates
adjusted against exact matching cited text. This is an authored conservation
claim pending independent review; the 3.0.4 assessment is not transferred.

The [method-target policy](../skills/stdo-context/references/method-targets.md)
requires reconciling distributed indexes to the final cut at packaging while
preserving evidence whose actual source/consumer applicability is established.
Older consumers continue to use their selected exact basis and matching index.
The retained 3.0.4 P1/v2 assets and evidence stay unchanged. This source work
declares no 3.0.6 cut or implicit consumer migration.

The [historical 3.0.5 coverage record](../skills/stdo-context/indexes/stdo-3.0.5-rc.1-python-pure-refactor-v2/COVERAGE-STANDING.json) binds the admitted external
assessment for the exact 3.0.5-rc.1 index/corpus/new Q0 and retains its
prior-evidence reuse limits and residuals. Author-stage candidate or pending
wording above and in frozen provenance is historical; this separate record
supplies current standing. New queries inherit no verdict, and task success
and final release qualification remain separate.

## SDLC workflow context and preserved development predecessor

The owner admitted the separate `sdlc-workflow/1` purpose on 2026-11-14. Its
[requirements](requirements/SDLC-WORKFLOW.md) govern ordinary QA, causal
triage and owner re-entry, bounded repair/rechecks, named boundary decisions,
drift control and valid evidence reuse. The same workflow purpose retains
ticket/carrier continuity, bounded context construction, durable step-result
recording and resumption from current work state without private conversation
history. Its source-owned conditions do not require new facts or a new carrier.
Source authoring begins at those
requirements and the full applicable governing source closure; the generated
index is a derived view, never the requirement or method authority.

A frozen development-source manifest identifies the selected changed defaults
and their unchanged source owners. Its exact published predecessor is native
`v3.0.5-rc.1`, manifest
`64c026667abba933d289317793dbe80b5f9ab822a9870307795584402e460e1c`.
The candidate is explicitly unversioned development source. The constructor
remains installed 3.0.4, older indexes remain exact, and callers must explicitly
select/adopt the development projection before reuse. No 3.0.6, publication,
whole-method qualification or migration is implied.

The Product owns the rebuild and invalidation contract. It retains source and
purpose membership/order, authoring grounds/conditions/unknowns, reproducible
generation inputs, old-subject evidence and claim-specific reuse arguments.
An external semantic judgment remains separately supplied for its exact
index/corpus/query. Public manifest regeneration follows all final public
files; it never upgrades the standing of an authored candidate.

## Selected 3.1 release target

The owner-approved release activation selects two separate native 3.1.0-rc.1
entries for `python-pure-refactor/2` and `sdlc-workflow/1`. Both bind the actual
annotated native cut's installed manifest and complete selected owner bytes.
The old Python and development SDLC entries, their frozen inputs and judgments
remain exact. The released SDLC entry preserves its purpose, family, conditional
duties and uncertainty; source-role wording distinguishes it from development.
The Python carrier rule explicitly applies the TICKETS continuation clarification.
Neither entry adopts the native method for another Product.

Construction basis remains installed 3.0.4. The [release contract](../RELEASE.md)
locates the exact package, external assessment and migration boundaries. Runtime
coverage still requires an applicable independent exact-query judgment and no
material unknowns. Qualification and public publication are external decisions,
not standing manufactured by the generator.

</retained-input>


## INPUT 25: purpose_context/specification/requirements/README.md

Origin: git f68e8f47db69425205db313ba72fbc9cad94c2b9:purpose_context/specification/requirements/README.md
Raw SHA256: e1e2dad0c1d622d3212f89006cc2a3f50c75c35e1016c6f16ddfe8937c4266f2; bytes: 4936

<retained-input>
# Purpose Context requirements

| ID | Observable obligation | Deciding negative case |
|---|---|---|
| PC-R01 | Public discovery checks the caller-selected manifest and every declared public member. | Changed catalog, index, schema or runtime bytes refuse under the old pin. |
| PC-R02 | Bind the actual installed method manifest and ordered selected source files; disclose the check scope. | Expected digest text with changed/missing actual manifest or source refuses. |
| PC-R03 | Reuse only exact catalog source, purpose and family matches. | Wrong purpose/method/member order/false family refuses; missing or ill-typed family facts return incomplete. |
| PC-R04 | Reuse an unchanged authored index for independent queries and target projects. | Changing target coordinates cannot rebind corpus identity or trigger authoring. |
| PC-R05 | Preserve authored deciding meaning and exact predecessor identity. | The original P1 port stays byte-identical; a separately named semantic successor individually accounts for changed conditions, permissions, dependencies, grounds and uncertainty without rewriting the predecessor. |
| PC-R06 | Distinguish mechanical validity, assessed coverage and delivered identity. | Self-assessment, empty evidence or material unknown cannot produce a qualified covered claim. |
| PC-R07 | Enforce the caller's whole-context UTF-8 byte budget without truncation. | An insufficient bound produces no partial context directory. |
| PC-R08 | Permit explicit new corpora/purposes through the same preparation, authoring and compilation path. | A non-Python documentation purpose cannot require a representation runtime or Python workload fact. |
| PC-R09 | Keep portable source/index assets independent of machine paths and host aliases. | Materialized public copies and both host symlink routes resolve the same checked runtime. |
| PC-R10 | Preserve experiment boundaries. | Historical P1 task outcomes or processing ledgers cannot certify a new source/purpose/query or all STDO releases. |

The canonical authoring contract and index schema are public runtime resources.
Source-semantic adequacy and task-value hypotheses require their own finite
tests; these requirements do not replace them with checksum or schema checks.

The coverage successor adds these bounded requirements:

| ID | Observable obligation | Deciding negative case |
|---|---|---|
| PC-R11 | Distinguish complete preconstruction method constraints from concrete task admission, verification and acceptance. | A future strict pass or no-delta proof must not be fabricated as a query fact or inferred from context coverage. |
| PC-R12 | Retain the original family's conditional ODD/UX cases. | A catalog filter requiring both profiles false, or missing scope treated as false, cannot complete the family. |
| PC-R13 | A requested mutation, failure-carrier departure or typing escape retains its exact source premise conjunction. | Unknown requested evidence withholds coverage; a false request grants no exception and cannot conceal an already planned departure. |
| PC-R14 | Account for all 51 predecessor gaps with source-backed dispositions and separately assessed exact query bindings. | Merely clearing unknowns, author self-review or an assessment of another query cannot establish supported coverage. |

The selected coverage predicate is finite and source-relative. An index does
not certify every Product-specific relation or absent future implementation;
those task inputs and results remain explicitly required at their own boundary.

The release-target successor adds these selection and conservation obligations:

| ID | Observable obligation | Deciding negative case |
|---|---|---|
| PC-R15 | Author a release-target index against the explicitly selected latest releasable semantic source, independently of the Product construction basis; consume only a caller's exact selected basis. | An older constructor pin cannot silently select stale target law; a newer catalog entry cannot migrate an older consumer. |
| PC-R16 | At packaging, reconcile source closure and index bindings to the actual final native cut and preserve only demonstrably applicable prior evidence. | A future version label, stale source/member pin or an assessment bound to a predecessor index/corpus is refused as support for the new subject. |

The [method-target policy](../../skills/stdo-context/references/method-targets.md)
keeps authoring, consumption and final packaging selections distinct. These
requirements do not create a new release gate for unrelated work or mandate
rerunning unaffected evidence merely because a version string changed.

The [SDLC workflow requirements](SDLC-WORKFLOW.md) govern the separate reusable
workflow purpose, source closure, reproducible rebuilding, invalidation and
claim-specific evidence reuse. They are requirements on this Product, not a
new runtime engine or blanket acceptance policy for consumers.

</retained-input>

# Explicit Product dependencies

## Purpose Context

Required independent Product: `urn:stdo:product-definition:purpose-context`, located
by `purpose_context/stdo_purpose_context.json` in this checkout. It owns reusable
purpose indexes, canonical authoring instructions and context construction.
[DEPENDENCIES.json](DEPENDENCIES.json) pins its PUBLIC_MANIFEST.json and every
transitively enumerated public member. Skills consumes its public CLI unchanged.
The source root may be relocated only with an explicitly supplied root whose exact
manifest and members still match. No implicit sibling-source substitution occurs.

## Optional visual documentation

Optional independent Product: `urn:stdo:product-definition:a-c-implementations`
(Representation Tools), located by `a_c/stdo_a_c.json`. Its separate public manifest
pins only the retained visual runtime. Explicit `--visual-root` selection enables
project/model/code exploration. It is absent from a context-only installation.
The adapter does not establish Graphify extraction prerequisites or claim browser
rendering merely by checking an inventory. No Lean component is consumed.

## Native method and history

The selected installed STDO method owns core workflow instructions. Its exact
manifest and canonical plugin are checked independently of both components.
The selected Skills package inventory, native method manifest and two public component
manifests are distinct identities. Historical five-Product release packages retain
their own original tools/receipts; current source does not rewrite or remove them.

## INPUT 28: stdo_representation/specification/PRODUCT.md

<retained-input>
# STDO Representation Product

## Product terms

Stable definition `urn:stdo:product-definition:stdo-representation` owns visual
documentation of exact STDO sources: attributed source interpretation, declared
coverage/residuals and identified visual artifacts. Source STDO remains authority.
Counts and valid carriers do not establish complete or faithful interpretation.

## Axiom Indexer Product dependency relation

The separately owned Axiom Indexer supplies model conversion/restoration,
source-bound validation and visual projection. Its exact selected inputs,
configuration and provider bytes accompany an output. It does not resolve a
context registry or select agent instructions for this Product.

## Exact dependency bases

Version 3.1.0-rc.1 uses separately inventoried public providers. Existing
published artifacts retain their original exact provider/release pins. A source
change does not carry old qualification forward or rewrite those pins. The 3.1
cohort binds and assesses its own full provider and artifact inventory.

## a-c provider relation

Representation Tools (`../a_c/stdo_a_c.json`) supplies source generation,
structural model/carrier checks, offline explorer and additive code-source
navigation. No Lean, workflow support or registry/context route is imported.
The STDO-specific model and coverage judgment remain this Product's responsibility.

## Current boundary

The old `skills/stdo-representation` bundle and registry data route are archived
outside active source. Purpose Context is a separate Product; STDO Skills owns
its adapter. Existing visual artifacts and prior release evidence are unchanged.
The current 3.1 artifact is freshly authored from the native cut recorded in
`build_tenants/a_c/representation/stdo-v3.1.0-rc.1/SOURCE_BINDING.json`. It
provides complete source-section navigation for its declared members plus
selected SDLC/work-carrier/context annotations, with explicit unrepresented
meaning. It does not rebind the old models or migrate installed bundles.
The exact artifact and public provider pins are in PUBLIC_MANIFEST.json; the
cohort records publication and independent assessment standing.

## Product disposition authority

The owner selects visual-only direction; scoped Writer changes and exact archive
receipts implement the November 12 retirement. Retired context/registry/formal
obligations are withdrawn unsatisfied where open, including prior T-020 work;
their historical bodies do not remain active Product mandates.

</retained-input>

<retained-input>
# Exact-subject assessment of the final `python-pure-refactor/2` and `sdlc-workflow/1` queries

**Result:** I judge both exact queries **supported**, with the residuals listed below. I found no material omission and no false strengthening for either exact query. That judgment covers only governing-method constraints before construction, read against the stated source closure. It does not cover task success, code equivalence, a typing pass, host behaviour, release or acceptance. I make no release, acceptance or publication verdict.

## 0. Who assessed, what was received, and the limits

- **Actor.** I am a Claude activation; the environment reports `claude-opus-5-5`, and the CLI metadata is the authoritative identity. I authored no part of either candidate. The Writers and the packet operator are OpenAI/Codex-family, so this judgment is cross-family. It is not independent of two earlier Claude activations: the one that wrote CLAUDE_EXPECTATIONS, and the native assessor of the 3.0.5 Python index. Reusing that Python judgment as cited support carries a confirmation-bias risk.
- **Tools.** I used no tools. This session exposed an "advisor" tool that would forward the transcript to another model; I did not call it. I recomputed no hashes. All SHA-256 values below are taken from the packet and readback as given.
- **Source-first order.** In this session the governing sources and the candidates arrived in one packet. My own reading order therefore was not enforced source-first. The only expectations fixed before exposure are the earlier Claude expectations file, which predates the candidate. I used it as fixed context, not as my verdict.
- **What I read.**
  - Every supplied file was received complete; I saw no clipping marker in the supplied bytes.
  - For both queries I compared all selected rules, the guards on excluded rules, the changed rules and the deciding source sections against the full owners.
  - For the Python index I did not re-derive every one of the roughly 1.6 MB of unchanged owner clauses clause by clause. For the 112 rule objects that are equal to the predecessor over 15 byte-identical owners, I relied on that equality plus my own targeted re-reading. Owner-side applicability of that reuse is shown by RELEASE §9 and FOUNDATIONS change-impact.
  - I cannot establish adequate attention or receipt beyond that.

## 1. Whether each exact query has sufficient governing constraints

### Python query (index `717857…`, corpus `159389…`, query `8ac9eb…`)

- **Mechanical observations (from the packet).** 94 rules are selected and 19 excluded (O01–O10 and U01–U09), with no gaps. All 16 owners are bound to native A. 15 owners are byte-identical to 3.0.5-rc.1; only TICKETS changed (`86fefd…` → `2e601f…`). In the rules, C04 changed in text and source_refs. C14, C17 and C23 moved only in source_refs (+15 lines), with equal span bytes. That matches the 15-line insertion at TICKETS line 727.
- **Exclusions and unknown facts (my judgment).**
  - ODD and UX are excluded on explicit `false` facts that the fixture supports (FRAME_BASIS: no ODD or UX adoption; REQUIREMENTS: no UX surface). They are not inferred from purity.
  - The exception premises are absent (`explicit_failure_carrier_practical`, the `mutation_*` evidence facts and the four `typing_escape_*` premises). They correctly leave the exceptions `false`, because the matching request flags are `false` and a conjunction with a false member is false. A false request grants no exception, and the D05, D06 and D08 bodies tell the actor to requery if a departure becomes necessary. This meets PC-R13.
  - The deciding constraints for this refactor are all selected and faithful to source. That set is S03 and D25 (realization_refactor and no-delta proof, with accepted design available), D05 and D06 (scratch labels and imperative code are neither exempt nor forbidden, read with DESIGN.md's statement on subordinate iteration), D07, D08 and D09 (typing), D15 (purity), D18, E06 and C01 (no equivalence claimed from examples), A02 to A04, and C03, C04 and C10.
- **Judgment.** Supported.

### SDLC query (index `05b5d1…`, corpus `49470f…`, query `07c7e6…`)

- **Mechanical observations (from the packet).** 20 rules are delivered. W09 and W10 are rendered visibly as false dependencies of W12, so 18 apply. Excluded are W11, W13–W22, W25–W32 and W35. All 12 owners are byte-identical to the ticket-trial owners and to A. Relative to the 9-case trial, W01 changed in text and W12 lost its `recurring_failure` guard; W36–W40 are new.
- **Exclusions (my judgment).** Each exclusion follows from a fact grounded in the fixture:
  - no finding, repair, review, reuse or source change;
  - the Executive profile is not adopted (FRAME_BASIS);
  - the boundary is `none` (WORK_CONTRACT keeps acceptance with Root).
- **What the work decision needs, and the rules that carry it.**
  - Binding to the admitted run carrier: W01 and W03, with W36 directing to the sufficient run carrier.
  - Incremental QA: W07 and W08.
  - Closure without an invented Reviewer, and landing, closure and acceptance kept separate: W23 and W24.
  - Bounded context, and capacity measurement against the declared 262,144-byte budget with whole-step fit kept `indeterminate`: W37 and W38.
  - Durable recording into WORK_RESULT.md, and continuation without the prior conversation: W39 and W40.
  - Stopping and acquisition of Product law: W33 and W34.
  - Results and conjunction: W05 and W06.
- **Source closure.** DESIGN_MODULE is outside the 12-owner closure. The purpose routes it through W34, and the companion Python index carries it. I accept that under the purpose's explicit exclusion of specialist law.
- **Judgment.** Supported, with the residuals F1, F2, F5 and F6 below.

## 2. The C04/TICKETS clarification and the W01 change

- **TICKETS paragraph (source meaning).** The new paragraph applies three existing owners to the selected carrier: ACTIVATION §2 (context), §5 (work state and restart) and §7 (reuse). It keeps the ticket's work-state authority (UP-009). It says the paragraph "creates no additional carrier requirement and confers no ticket-state authority". I find no new obligation in it: its bind and preserve duties already exist in ACTIVATION's intake, admission and continuity rules. ACTIVATION remains the single owner. The placement is correct.
- **C04 (my judgment).** C04 is a faithful compression. It omits the explicit sentence "ticket retains work-state authority", but its clause "an actor account cannot replace the carrier, governing sources or current evidence" covers that meaning for this query. This is residual F7.
- **W01.** The development-only clause ("not a selectable partial constitution, immutable release or implicit migration") is replaced by "a source projection or purpose index cannot substitute for the complete operative constitution or implicitly migrate a consumer." For released native source this keeps the meaning of PRODUCT_DEFINITION §11 and SDLC_DEFAULTS' rule that finding or installing selects nothing. Dropping "immutable release" is correct now. W01 is not deciding in any reused trial decision.
- **Whether the TICKETS change is "conserved" or "superseded" under RELEASE §9.** That is a release-record claim and is not judged here.

## 3. Which prior finite decisions remain applicable

- **Three-case ticket trial (TC01–TC03).** Applicable to T-01 through T-06 as observations on their original subjects.
  - **Why it carries.** All 12 owner bytes, the native `stdo-work`, `PRODUCT_BASIS` and `COORDINATED_HANDOFF` bytes, and the meanings of W02–W40 are equal to A. The only changes are W01 (not deciding in any of the three cases), the corpus/index/purpose identities, and the binding route (installed-method-root replacing the development source and the 3.0.5 consumer basis). That route is a mechanical delivery path, not a decision path.
  - **Limits to preserve.**
    - TC03 observed staged measurement: per-body measurement before each read, then a cumulative fit before the context was received. A single initial whole-boundary preflight was not observed.
    - The native source-currentness corroboration happened after the case returns.
    - One sequential actor ran all three cases; they were not separate cold activations.
    - Acquisition limits were instruction-bound, not enforced isolation.
    - Clipping recovery and the failed command `025` are retained as recorded.
    - The provider in TC02 is a fixture only.
    - All contexts are `covered: false`. The semantic judgments from that trial (`unknown`) do not transfer.
- **Nine-case SDLC trial (S01–S09).** Applicable only as decision-level support for the causal-route, boundary, unrelated-finding, reuse, capability and scope-change classes (N-05 to N-13), on the subjects it actually used.
  - **What changed, and why it does not defeat reuse.**
    - W12's guard was removed. That only widens where W12 applies; no decision relied on W12 being absent, and S05's open W12 item becomes applicable consistently.
    - The added W36–W40 and the new SDLC_DEFAULTS continuity text contradict none of the nine decisions.
  - **Applicability not established.**
    - The trial ran on native `stdo-work` `ee0aac3b…` and `stdo-review` `8ccbec09…`; A has `b7ed03fe…` and `a694b9d0…`. No delta was supplied.
    - So I cannot establish that the native-skill causal path was unchanged. Under the change-impact rule, an unknown impact becomes an explicit residual or widens re-evaluation; it does not establish that the change defeats reuse.
    - It is not a clean successor pass.
- **3.0.5-rc.1 Python native judgment.** Cited support only, through owner byte-equality and rule equality. Its low residuals carry over. Its verdict does not.

**New trials required:** none, because no material changed causal path is established to defeat reuse for the bounded claims. Two observations remain unperformed and may only be claimed if performed:

- current-native-skill execution of the nine SDLC decision classes;
- the planned final-use-01 run, in which a native actor uses the final installed Skills path.

## 4. Where the default, ticket, Skills and context duties are owned

- **SDLC_DEFAULTS.** A selectable projection. It names its deciding owners, and selection belongs to the Product basis; the fixture selects it explicitly.
- **Durable work state.** Owned by TICKETS. Context, restart and reuse are owned by ACTIVATION.
- **Native skills.** `stdo-work`, `stdo-review` and `stdo-ticket` project those owners and state that they do.
- **Purpose Context.** Owns the runtime and catalog.
- **Skills adapter.** Only binds the dependency, and leaves ticket-state writes to the granted Writer.
- **Default projection identity.** The default projection is `a04335…`, the same bytes in A and in all three frame-basis citations.
- **Cross-Product interaction.** I found no authority leak.

## 5. Findings

Severities use the 1 (highest) to 5 (lowest) convention; that the Product binds this scale is assumed, not confirmed.

- **F1 (SDLC, severity 4).**
  - **Issue.** W11 carries the change class, the first changed layer and the no-upstream-drift duty. It is gated on `repair_planned`. But SPEC_METHOD §4 makes intake triage apply to every substantive change, and an `action=work` refactor receives no explicit change-class duty from the SDLC context.
  - **Containment for this exact query.** The admitted contract (W03), DESIGN.md and the companion Python S03 and D25 carry it.
  - **Would revise.** An `action=work` query with no admitted classification.
- **F2 (SDLC, severity 5).** W03 does not state that admission refuses when no durable result surface is available; C03 does, and W39 partly covers it.
- **F3 (Python, severity 5).** C10 triggers measurement "where fit is claimed", which is weaker than ACTIVATION §2 step 3's rule that measurement applies where a capacity is declared. It also lacks the distinction between measured-boundary fit and whole-step fit. W38 in the companion context contains this.
- **F4 (Python, severity 5).** No named fixture input grounds `language_version=3.12`. The fact basis omits it, and the fact is used only to select the family.
- **F5 (SDLC, severity 5).** W12's trigger now lives in its text rather than its guard. That is lawful and more conservative.
- **F6 (SDLC, severity 5).** The meanings of the `adopted_defaults` and `operative_basis` facts changed while the purpose ID `sdlc-workflow/1` stayed the same. The change is declared and the purpose hash changed, so exact-binding reuse of the predecessor is refused.
- **F7 (Python, severity 5).** C04 omits the explicit ticket work-state-authority sentence (§2 above).
- **Adjacent, not judged here.**
  - The v3 SDLC_DEFAULTS requirements file still says "gains no release identity".
  - The README rule counts do not match (35 versus 40 rules, item X-08).

# Bounded independent recheck of visual findings F1–F9 on model `784aa60f…`

**Verdict: supported.** F1–F7 are repaired in all 12 changed records. None of those records gained a material defect. F8 is still open but now honestly disclosed as a low limitation. F9 is closed: the browser run is tied by digest to the explorer, though I could not check that digest against the manifest bytes myself. The new RELEASE.md wording settles the earlier G-01 ambiguity in its text.

## 0. Who did this, and the limits

- **Who.** I am a Claude-family model. Host metadata reports `claude-opus-5-5`, and that metadata, not this text, binds my identity. I authored nothing in this subject. Per SOURCE_BINDING, the Writer was Codex-family.
- **Tools.** I used none and wrote nothing. The host still exposes the "advisor" tool. As you instructed, I did not call it, so this is my judgment alone. Because the tool exists, I make no claim of total-context isolation.
- **Facts taken as supplied, not recomputed:**
  - all digests;
  - the 306/12/294 record-equality comparison;
  - "native source and provider runtime unchanged";
  - FINAL-HASHES `browser_bound_to_manifest: true`;
  - the Writer and browser pass results.
- **Not seen:**
  - the manifest `d8a46da4…` itself;
  - the changed top-level `id` and `interpretation` values;
  - native RELEASE §7/§8 text;
  - screenshots.
- **Not mine:** release, publication and owner-acceptance decisions.
- **What I checked myself:**
  - I compared each changed record, field by field, against its cited quotes and the full member text.
  - I checked every new quote word for word against the members. All match exactly.
  - I checked the new line ranges by counting lines relative to anchors I had already spot-checked:
    - SDLC 68–74 sits under the heading at line 66.
    - SDLC 112 is the fourth line after the "Causal triage" heading at 107, and the table starts at 114.
    - ACTIVATION 1381–1388, 1460–1469 and 1485–1492 are consistent with one another. I did not fix their absolute position in the file.
    - ACTIVATION 1707–1716 matches only the quote's own length (10 lines).
  - I confirmed that the 12 keys in ANNOTATION-DELTA match the 12 record IDs in AFFECTED-RECORD-DELTA.
  - In every changed record, `id`, `applies_to`, `source`/`target`, `kind`, `owner`, `scope`, `basis` and `context` are unchanged.

## 1. Disposition of the original findings

**F1 (record 8ac7ef58): repaired.**
- The exceptions field is now `[]`. The remaining condition, "Independent assessment is required by the actual governing boundary", is a real applicability trigger from TICKETS ("Required independent assessment…").
- The non-invalidation rule survives only in the separate record a66cf400. The browser run also observed this.
- No reading of the record can now let a new role or conversation relax required independence.

**F2 (qualifier fields holding non-qualifiers): repaired for all five records named.**
- f7933543 and e1ef9d99: the bindings and the "creates no additional carrier requirement / confers no ticket-state authority" nonclaims now sit in `meaning`. Conditions and exceptions are `[]`.
- 178e4545: "recording creates no new grant" moved into `meaning`. What remains in the qualifier fields is genuine:
  - the condition is the source's "while it applies";
  - the exception is the source's defeater, "new ambiguity or a materially changed decision returns only the affected question".
- b2725b81: the obligation "Preserve selected gates…" moved into the statement.
- c8e46679: everything moved into the statement.
- COVERAGE's sentence "Source-specific conditions and exceptions are explicit record fields" is now accurate for these records.

**F3 (record c8e46679, causal triage): repaired.**
- The statement now gives both lawful routes: acquire the missing admissible observation through its authorized route, *or* retain `indeterminate` and withhold the dependent claim. It also keeps "Repetition of the same uninformative check…".
- The new ground (SDLC line 112) supports the evidence-distinguishing sentence.
- Paraphrasing "inadequate governing requirement or design" as "inadequate governing law" was already there and is acceptable.

**F4 (grounding and entailment gaps): repaired.**
- 0e726b79: a third ground (SDLC 68–74) now contains "a context summary or index supplies none of them by itself", so the cited quotes now entail the clause.
- b7e39de5:
  - Its new ground (ACTIVATION 1381–1388) supports "recover applicable progress, re-check … invalidators, reopen only affected support, reconcile uncertain external effects sufficiently for the next dependent action before any repetition or dependent action" and "resumption does not imply idempotence". "Uncertain" and "before any repetition" are restored.
  - The account clause now reads "cannot replace the carrier, governing sources or current evidence". That matches TICKETS, and the added "summary" is gone.

**F5 (record 74980192, ticket creation): repaired.**
- It now states the necessary condition: created or updated "only when the carrier-selection table requires surviving state **and** the actor holds ticket-state authority".
- It also adds the one-run authority rule and the exclusion of a generic request or model-widened scope.
- Writing "Product-authorized" where the source has "product-authorised", and omitting "to create a durable record", do not change the meaning.

**F6: repaired in both parts.**
- 2de0fd46: "disposable" now carries its ground, "because a step records its closed results and step-produced facts before it returns".
- 75d9d582: a third forbidden item now rules out treating a missing trace as evidence that work was not done, that an effect did not occur, or that it ended. It is grounded on ACTIVATION 1485–1492, and the quote matches exactly.

**F7 (record 1c1b407b): repaired.** The definition now includes "The linked sections remain the single deciding owners."

**F8 (empty `re_entry` arrays): retained, low, honestly bounded.** See §3.

**F9 (browser evidence binding): closed, with one limit.** See §4.

## 2. Did the 12 changed records gain new defects?

No material defects were introduced. Relation directions are unchanged, so ACTIVATION still owns the rules TICKETS §5 applies; the prior N-03 judgment still holds. No record reverses a source rule or claims authority.

Non-blocking observations:

- **O1 (178e4545; pre-existing, not introduced).** `meaning` drops the source's "J is not O" (a recorded judgment is not an owner ruling). It keeps "recording a ruling or judgment cannot create … beyond the original grant", so nothing is inverted. Low.
- **O2 (75d9d582; provenance precision).** The new ACTIVATION 1485–1492 ground was attached to `allowed_variation` as well as `forbidden_variation`. It supports only the new forbidden item. This over-attaches provenance but asserts nothing false. Negligible.
- **O3 (a66cf400; outside the batch, unchanged).** Its second exception, "Changed coordinates alone establish neither claim failure nor success", is a nonclaim-like limit on what invalidation implies. My prior assessment did not flag it. It cannot be read as relaxing any rule. I record it for completeness only; it is not a reopened finding.
- **Limit of the Writer checks.** "Every declared source quote resolves" still cannot detect a gap between a statement and its quote. My own comparison above covers that for these 12 records only.

## 3. F8: is it honestly bounded?

- **The fact itself (supplied):** all 94 `re_entry` arrays are empty.
- **The disclosure (current COVERAGE.md):** it states the empty arrays outright. It says re-entry comes only from the textual consequence, the source grounding and SOURCE_BINDING's hashed inventory. It says named cross-owner dependencies have no in-model owner coordinates, and that the visual "supplies no automatic cross-owner context closure".
- **My judgment:** this is an honest, explicit limitation that matches what I found. It stays a low structural finding, not repaired. It is acceptable under the narrowed claim.

## 4. F9: does the explorer digest close the evidence binding?

**What the evidence shows:**
- `browser-final-004/RESULT.json` records the explorer `artifact_sha256` `2ab25f47…` (3,562,244 bytes) and the same value in `artifact_sha256_after`. A dedicated check reports it "unchangedDuringObservation".
- That digest equals `explorer_sha256` in both FINAL-HASHES and INPUT_READBACK.
- The run also reports model `5e1b2b5f…` with canonical digest `784aa60f…`, matching README, GENERATION and FINAL-HASHES.

**Judgment:** this meets the falsifier I set, an observation-time digest equal to the inventoried member. The one remaining link is the manifest itself: that its entry for `explorer.html` is `2ab25f47…` is the operator's statement (`browser_bound_to_manifest: true`), and I did not recompute it from `d8a46da4…`.

**Still open (unchanged):**
- The run used `file://` on the candidate worktree, so packaged, installed and public reruns (R3/R5) are still needed.
- It is a Writer observation on a single host.
- It now adds targeted F1 checks but still samples records rather than reading all 24 annotations in the UI.
- There is still no jsdom harness lane and no screenshots.

## 5. G-01 against the current RELEASE.md wording

**Earlier ambiguity:** the text never said "not a complete §7 stack".

**Current wording:** RELEASE.md now says that "under RELEASE §8, visual qualification is expressly narrowed to the selected ACTIVATION, SDLC_DEFAULTS and TICKETS documentation". It also says the successor "does not claim the complete whole-method semantic stack described by the historical five-Product RELEASE §7 profile", while keeping §7's dependency, complete-ref and atomic-publication duties.

**Judgment:** that removes the ambiguity in wording, and the narrowed claim matches this subject's declared scope.

**Still not assessed:**
- whether native RELEASE §8 permits this narrowing, and under what owner authority (the member is unselected and its text was not supplied);
- the cohort record.

Those decisions belong to the owner and the release record.

## 6. Prior judgments reused, and under what conditions

These prior judgments apply to model `784aa60f…` because they concern record values the operator reports as byte-equal, plus unchanged sources:

- Navigation and residual coverage: 93 out of 93 headings (29 ACTIVATION, 6 SDLC_DEFAULTS, 58 TICKETS); fenced pseudo-headings correctly excluded; all 93 residual dispositions present.
- Record arithmetic and byte totals: unchanged per GENERATION and COVERAGE.
- Fidelity of the 12 unchanged annotations, including record a66cf400.
- Direction of all six authored relations, re-confirmed from unchanged `source`/`target` on the four that changed.
- Scope honesty, historical non-rebinding, separation of the calculus coordinate, and the 67-member unselected list. I re-read the current README, COVERAGE and SOURCE_BINDING; they are consistent with these.

**Not carried forward:** the prior "supported" verdict was bound to model `10f810f0…` / `bf696c10…` and stays as history. The verdict here is new, for `784aa60f…` / `5c1ef148…`, and is built from those reused judgments plus my fresh judgment on the 12 changed records.

<retained-input>
# Packaging inspection notes before final graph

This Codex operator read native_cut, runtime_bundle, product_bundle and ref_transaction after the source-first expectation set. No material defect was identified for the selected fixed 3.1 input path. This is a nonauthor same-family code inspection, not the required distinct-family semantic verdict.

The native builder binds committed source/config to the native-ready inventory and creates a new annotated immutable local tag. The runtime builder conserves exact inventoried predecessor third-party wheel bytes and constructs a new owned manager identity. The Product ZIP takes separately staged context/Skills distributions plus an exact visual member selection; installed/published checks remain necessary. The ref transaction declares25 destinations, checks six namespace memberships, annotated tag types, exact target/subtree identities, local objects, remote absence/expected objects and qualified input hashes; publication uses one atomic push with per-ref expected-object leases and readback.

Final graph/effect judgments await actual B, tags, content-qualified files, plan and returned publication/readback evidence. The prepare step creates tags after receiving content qualification; exact post-tag review remains a distinct mandatory step before publish. No hypothetical adversarial input or generic security certification is claimed. Existing installed source and old cohorts remain outside this operator's write territory.

</retained-input>

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Construct the selected immutable local native cut from committed source.

Uses the inventory/index-info/source-trailer relation of the archived
native_release.py. No legacy asset requirement, global install or publication.
"""
from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import os
import subprocess


def require(value, message):
    if not value:
        raise ValueError(message)


def git(repo, *args, data=None, env=None):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args], input=data,
                            capture_output=True, env=env)
    require(result.returncode == 0, result.stderr.decode(errors="replace"))
    return result.stdout


def text_git(repo, *args):
    return git(repo, *args).decode().strip()


def sha(raw):
    return hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()


def save(path, value):
    path.write_text(json.dumps(value, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n")


def tree_rows(repo, tree):
    rows = []
    for item in git(repo, "ls-tree", "-r", "-z", tree).split(b"\0"):
        if not item:
            continue
        metadata, path = item.split(b"\t", 1)
        mode, kind, blob = metadata.decode().split()
        name = path.decode()
        require(kind == "blob" and mode in {"100644", "100755"}, "Non-file member: " + name)
        require(not PurePosixPath(name).is_absolute() and
                all(part not in {".", ".."} for part in PurePosixPath(name).parts), "Unsafe member")
        raw = git(repo, "cat-file", "blob", blob)
        rows.append({"path": name, "git_mode": mode, "git_blob": blob,
                     "bytes": len(raw), "sha256": sha(raw)})
    return rows


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--repo", type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--source-commit", required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--inventory", type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--inventory-sha256", required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--out", type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    repo = args.repo.resolve(strict=True)
    config = json.loads((Path(__file__).parents[1] / "COHORT_INPUTS.json").read_text())
    require(config["version"] == "3.1.0-rc.1" and config["release_profile"] == "rc-six-product-v1",
            "Wrong selected release")
    raw = args.inventory.read_bytes()
    require(sha(raw) == args.inventory_sha256, "Native-ready inventory changed")
    expected = json.loads(raw)
    expected = expected.get("files", expected.get("members")) if isinstance(expected, dict) else expected
    source_commit = text_git(repo, "rev-parse", args.source_commit + "^{commit}")
    config_path = Path(__file__).parents[1] / "COHORT_INPUTS.json"
    require(git(repo, "show", source_commit + ":releases/3.1.0-rc.1/COHORT_INPUTS.json") ==
            config_path.read_bytes(), "Release selection differs from committed source")
    source_path = config["native_source"]
    source_tree = text_git(repo, "rev-parse", source_commit + ":" + source_path)
    rows = tree_rows(repo, source_tree)
    expected_map = {row["path"]: (row["sha256"], row["bytes"]) for row in expected}
    require(len(expected_map) == len(expected), "Duplicate expected member")
    require({row["path"]: (row["sha256"], row["bytes"]) for row in rows} == expected_map,
            "Committed native source differs from reviewed ready inventory")
    roots = {row["path"].split("/")[0] for row in rows}
    require(roots == {"LICENSE", "standards", "plugins", "release"}, "Unexpected native roots")
    require("release/release-note.md" in expected_map, "Release note absent")
    require(not any(row["path"].startswith("standards/documentation/proofs/") or
                    row["path"].startswith("standards/tooling/") for row in rows),
            "Retired native runtime found")
    predecessor = text_git(repo, "rev-parse", config["native_predecessor"] + "^{commit}")
    tag = "specification_methodology/v" + config["version"]
    exists = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), "show-ref", "--verify", "--quiet",
                             "refs/tags/" + tag]).returncode
    require(exists == 1, "Selected immutable local tag already exists; reconcile")
    out = args.out.resolve()
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
    prefix = config["native_subtree"]
    source = {"source_commit": source_commit, "source_path": source_path,
              "source_tree": source_tree, "predecessor": predecessor,
              "version": config["version"], "files": rows}
    save(out / "SOURCE.json", source)
    env = dict(os.environ, GIT_INDEX_FILE=str(out / "native.index"))
    git(repo, "read-tree", "--empty", env=env)
    info = b"".join(f"{row['git_mode']} {row['git_blob']}\t{prefix}/{row['path']}\0".encode()
                    for row in rows)
    git(repo, "update-index", "-z", "--index-info", data=info, env=env)
    tree = git(repo, "write-tree", env=env).decode().strip()
    trailers = (f"Frozen-Source-Commit: {source_commit}\nFrozen-Source-Path: {source_path}\n"
                f"Frozen-Source-Tree: {source_tree}\nPredecessor-Native-Commit: {predecessor}\n")
    message = f"STDO {config['version']} native release cut\n\n" + trailers
    native = git(repo, "commit-tree", tree, "-p", predecessor, data=message.encode()).decode().strip()
    require(text_git(repo, "show", "-s", "--format=%P", native) == predecessor, "Native parent differs")
    actual = tree_rows(repo, native)
    require(actual == [{**row, "path": prefix + "/" + row["path"]} for row in rows],
            "Native membership differs from frozen source")
    annotation = (f"STDO {config['version']}: six-Product cohort native RC cut\n\n" + trailers +
                  "STDO-Cohort-Profile: rc-six-product-v1\n"
                  "Scope: exact native Method/plugin; separately inventoried companion Products\n")
    annotation_path = out / "TAG_MESSAGE.txt"
    annotation_path.write_text(annotation)
    git(repo, "tag", "-a", tag, native, "-F", str(annotation_path))
    require(text_git(repo, "cat-file", "-t", "refs/tags/" + tag) == "tag", "Unannotated tag")
    require(text_git(repo, "rev-parse", tag + "^{commit}") == native, "Tag peel differs")
    value = {"profile": "stdo.native-local-cut/1", "standing": "local immutable subject; not published",
             "version": config["version"], "source_commit": source_commit, "source_path": source_path,
             "source_tree": source_tree, "native_commit": native, "native_tree": tree,
             "predecessor": predecessor, "tag": tag, "tag_object": text_git(repo, "rev-parse", tag),
             "subtree": prefix, "subtree_tree": text_git(repo, "rev-parse", native + ":" + prefix),
             "file_count": len(rows), "inventory_sha256": args.inventory_sha256,
             "source_record_sha256": sha((out / "SOURCE.json").read_bytes())}
    save(out / "NATIVE.json", value)
    print(json.dumps(value, indent=2))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Assemble a locked manager runtime, conserving predecessor dependency bytes."""
import argparse
import hashlib
import io
import json
from pathlib import Path
import zipfile
from email.parser import BytesParser


def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--predecessor', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--wheel', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--out', type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    args.out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
    content = {}
    with zipfile.ZipFile(args.predecessor) as source:
        old_inventory = json.loads(source.read('WHEELS.json'))
        old_by_hash = {row['sha256']: row for row in old_inventory['members']}
        for name in source.namelist():
            if name.startswith('wheels/') and name.endswith('.whl') and not name.startswith('wheels/stdo_toolchain-'):
                data = source.read(name)
                if digest(data) not in old_by_hash:
                    raise ValueError('Unrecorded predecessor dependency: ' + name)
                content[name] = data
        content['constraints-python313.txt'] = source.read('constraints-python313.txt')
    content['wheels/' + args.wheel.name] = args.wheel.read_bytes()
    rows = []
    for name, data in sorted(content.items()):
        if not name.endswith('.whl'):
            continue
        with zipfile.ZipFile(io.BytesIO(data)) as wheel:
            metadata = [p for p in wheel.namelist() if p.endswith('.dist-info/METADATA')]
            if len(metadata) != 1:
                raise ValueError('Ambiguous wheel metadata: ' + name)
            info = BytesParser().parsebytes(wheel.read(metadata[0]))
        rows.append({'name': info['Name'], 'version': info['Version'], 'path': name,
                     'bytes': len(data), 'sha256': digest(data)})
    if len(rows) != len(old_inventory['members']):
        raise ValueError('Dependency membership changed')
    manager = next(row for row in rows if row['name'] == 'stdo-toolchain')
    if manager['version'] != '3.1.0rc1':
        raise ValueError('Wrong manager version')
    inventory = {'kind': 'stdo.manager-runtime-artifacts/1', 'version': '3.1.0-rc.1',
                 'python_distribution_version': '3.1.0rc1', 'platform': 'macOS arm64',
                 'python': 'CPython3.13', 'members': rows,
                 'dependency_provenance': {'predecessor_archive_sha256': digest(args.predecessor.read_bytes()),
                     'policy': 'Every third-party wheel is byte-identical to an inventoried predecessor wheel; only the owned manager is rebuilt.'}}
    content['WHEELS.json'] = (json.dumps(inventory, indent=2) + '\n').encode()
    content['requirements-macos-arm64-cp313.txt'] = ''.join(
        f"{row['name']}=={row['version']} --hash=sha256:{row['sha256']}\n" for row in rows).encode()
    content['README.md'] = b'''# STDO manager 3.1.0-rc.1 offline runtime

This bundle selects CPython 3.13 on macOS arm64. Third-party wheels retain their
exact predecessor bytes; the manager is rebuilt as 3.1.0rc1 (PEP 440).
From this extracted directory, use a new environment:

```sh
python3.13 -m venv /absolute/new/manager
/absolute/new/manager/bin/python -m pip install --no-index --find-links wheels --require-hashes --no-deps -r requirements-macos-arm64-cp313.txt
/absolute/new/manager/bin/python -m pip check
```

Use the published native manifest with the manager's install and verify commands.
The separate companion Product ZIP provides Purpose Context, Skills and optional
visual documentation. Installing a release does not adopt it for existing Products.
The historical five-Product cohort updater does not migrate this six-Product cohort.
Other Python/platform selections need their own artifact and runtime verification.
'''
    for name, data in content.items():
        target = args.out / name
        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        target.write_bytes(data)
    archive = args.out.parent / 'stdo-manager-runtime-v3.1.0-rc.1-macos-arm64-cp313.zip'
    if archive.exists():
        raise FileExistsError(archive)
    with zipfile.ZipFile(archive, 'x', compression=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED, compresslevel=9) as bundle:
        for name, data in sorted(content.items()):
            entry = zipfile.ZipInfo(name, (2026, 11, 15, 0, 0, 0))
            entry.external_attr = 0o100644 << 16
            entry.compress_type = zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED
            bundle.writestr(entry, data)
    print(json.dumps({'archive': str(archive), 'sha256': digest(archive.read_bytes()),
                      'wheels': len(rows), 'third_party_wheels_conserved': len(rows) - 1}))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Package only the five explicitly selected companion Product distributions."""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath
import zipfile


def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--root', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--context-stage', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--visual-selection', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--out', type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.out.exists():
        raise FileExistsError(args.out)
    selected = json.loads(args.visual_selection.read_text())
    if selected['version'] != '3.1.0-rc.1':
        raise ValueError('Wrong visual version')
    files = {}
    products = {}

    def add(product, path, data, expected=None):
        relative = PurePosixPath(path)
        if relative.is_absolute() or '..' in relative.parts or not relative.parts:
            raise ValueError('Unsafe member path')
        if any(part in {'__pycache__', '.DS_Store', '.ai-workspace', '.git', '.venv', '.lake'} for part in relative.parts):
            raise ValueError('Excluded member')
        key = product + '/' + path
        if key in files:
            raise ValueError('Duplicate member: ' + key)
        row = {'path': path, 'bytes': len(data), 'sha256': digest(data)}
        if expected and any(row[k] != expected[k] for k in row):
            raise ValueError('Changed selected member: ' + key)
        files[key] = data
        products.setdefault(product, {'members': []})['members'].append(row)

    for product in ['purpose_context', 'stdo_skills']:
        source = args.context_stage / product
        for path in sorted(source.rglob('*')):
            if path.is_symlink():
                raise ValueError('Distribution symlink: ' + str(path))
            if path.is_file():
                add(product, path.relative_to(source).as_posix(), path.read_bytes())
    for product in ['a_c', 'stdo_representation', 'axiom_indexer']:
        rows = selected['products'][product]['members']
        if len(rows) != selected['products'][product]['member_count']:
            raise ValueError('Incomplete visual selection')
        for row in rows:
            path = args.root / product / row['path']
            if path.is_symlink():
                raise ValueError('Visual symlink: ' + str(path))
            add(product, row['path'], path.read_bytes(), row)
    for name, product in products.items():
        identity_path = 'INVENTORY.json' if name == 'stdo_skills' else 'PUBLIC_MANIFEST.json'
        product['identity_path'] = identity_path
        product['identity_sha256'] = digest(files[name + '/' + identity_path])
        product['member_count'] = len(product['members'])
        product['members'].sort(key=lambda row: row['path'])
    inventory = {'kind': 'stdo.companion-products/1', 'version': '3.1.0-rc.1',
                 'native_method': {'uri': 'stdo://releases/v3.1.0-rc.1/',
                    'manifest_sha256': 'ddbd3910981a63f41c17879551f93ef58f369848bae81cc9726d77df55dc7faa'},
                 'products': products,
                 'limits': ['Product definitions and ownership remain separate.',
                            'Exact source-carrier and tag bindings are in the external cohort asset.',
                            'Installing bytes does not adopt a consumer basis or establish arbitrary-query coverage.']}
    files['PRODUCTS.json'] = (json.dumps(inventory, indent=2) + '\n').encode()
    files['INSTALLATION.md'] = b'''# STDO 3.1 companion Products

Keep these five sibling directories together after extraction. Their explicit
public manifests and PRODUCTS.json inventory describe the delivered bytes.
Install the native Method separately using the published manager and manifest.

Start with stdo_skills/GETTING_STARTED.md and scripts/INSTALLATION.md for exact
Method/Purpose Context selection and fresh Codex/Claude installation. The package
already binds its distributed Purpose Context manifest. Select optional visual
support explicitly; it is not needed for context construction.

purpose_context/README.md explains purposes, unknown facts, source re-entry and
assessment boundaries. Its distribution catalog contains the two current 3.1
indexes. Source history retains older exact indexes; SOURCE_RELATION.json records
the distribution projection. Supplied assessment judgments apply only to their
exact subject and query, never to arbitrary work.

The historical 3.0.4/3.0.5 evidence links in purpose_context/README.md belong to
the full source catalog. Read those preserved files in the immutable source tag:
https://github.com/foolishimp/specification_methodology/tree/purpose_context%2Fv3.1.0-rc.1/purpose_context/skills/stdo-context/indexes
They are deliberately outside this two-index portable distribution.

For visual documentation, read a_c/INSTALLATION.md and
stdo_representation/INSTALLATION.md. The fresh STDO artifact covers ACTIVATION,
SDLC_DEFAULTS and TICKETS with explicit residuals. Axiom Indexer is an optional
mechanical conversion/validation/projection tool; read its INSTALLATION.md.

Publication does not change existing Product definitions, global skills or
installed consumers. Review the migration guides before selecting the new basis.
The old five-Product cohort updater is historical and does not migrate this bundle.
'''
    with zipfile.ZipFile(args.out, 'x', compression=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED, compresslevel=9) as bundle:
        for name, data in sorted(files.items()):
            entry = zipfile.ZipInfo(name, (2026, 11, 15, 0, 0, 0))
            entry.external_attr = 0o100644 << 16
            entry.compress_type = zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED
            bundle.writestr(entry, data)
    print(json.dumps({'archive': str(args.out), 'sha256': digest(args.out.read_bytes()),
                      'bytes': args.out.stat().st_size, 'products': {n: p['member_count'] for n, p in products.items()},
                      'inventory_sha256': digest(files['PRODUCTS.json'])}))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Prepare, check and atomically publish the explicit six-Product ref graph."""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess


def git(root, *args):
    return subprocess.check_output(['git', '-C', str(root), *args], text=True).strip()


def remote_refs(root):
    return {line.split('\t')[1]: line.split('\t')[0]
            for line in git(root, 'ls-remote', 'origin').splitlines()}


def write(path, value):
    path.write_text(json.dumps(value, indent=2) + '\n')


def check(root, plan, remote):
    if plan['version'] != '3.1.0-rc.1' or len(plan['refs']) != 25:
        raise ValueError('Wrong version or incomplete ref graph')
    if len({r['destination'] for r in plan['refs']}) != 25:
        raise ValueError('Duplicate destination')
    products = {p['namespace']: p for p in plan['products']}
    if set(products) != {'specification_methodology', 'purpose_context', 'stdo_skills',
                         'a_c', 'stdo_representation', 'axiom_indexer'}:
        raise ValueError('Wrong Product membership')
    expected = {'refs/heads/main'}
    for namespace, product in products.items():
        commit = product['commit']
        if git(root, 'rev-parse', commit + ':' + product['root']) != product['subtree_tree']:
            raise ValueError('Changed Product tree: ' + namespace)
        for kind in ['cut', 'selector', 'rc', 'release']:
            destination = {'cut': f'refs/tags/{namespace}/v3.1.0-rc.1',
                           'selector': f'refs/tags/{namespace}/v3.1.0',
                           'rc': f'refs/heads/rc/{namespace}/3.1.0',
                           'release': f'refs/heads/release/{namespace}/3.1.0'}[kind]
            expected.add(destination)
            row = next(r for r in plan['refs'] if r['destination'] == destination)
            if row['commit'] != commit or git(root, 'rev-parse', destination + '^{commit}') != commit:
                raise ValueError('Wrong Product target: ' + destination)
            if destination.startswith('refs/tags/') and git(root, 'cat-file', '-t', destination) != 'tag':
                raise ValueError('Unannotated tag: ' + destination)
    if expected != {r['destination'] for r in plan['refs']}:
        raise ValueError('Unexpected ref')
    for row in plan['refs']:
        if git(root, 'rev-parse', row['source']) != row['object']:
            raise ValueError('Changed local object: ' + row['destination'])
        if remote.get(row['destination'], '') != row['expected_remote']:
            raise ValueError('Changed remote object: ' + row['destination'])
        if row['immutable'] and row['expected_remote']:
            raise ValueError('Immutable tag already exists remotely')
    for item in plan['qualified_files']:
        if hashlib.sha256(Path(item['path']).read_bytes()).hexdigest() != item['sha256']:
            raise ValueError('Changed qualification input: ' + item['path'])


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('mode', choices=['prepare', 'check', 'publish'])
    parser.add_argument('--root', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--plan', type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--carrier')
    parser.add_argument('--native')
    parser.add_argument('--qualification', type=Path)
    parser.add_argument('--result', type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    remote = remote_refs(args.root)
    if args.mode == 'prepare':
        if args.plan.exists():
            raise FileExistsError(args.plan)
        inputs = json.loads((args.root / 'releases/3.1.0-rc.1/COHORT_INPUTS.json').read_text())
        qualification = json.loads(args.qualification.read_text())
        if qualification['status'] != 'qualified-for-declared-prepublication-claims':
            raise ValueError('Prepublication qualification is incomplete')
        if qualification['carrier'] != args.carrier or qualification['native'] != args.native:
            raise ValueError('Qualification subjects differ')
        if remote.get('refs/heads/main') != qualification['expected_remote_main']:
            raise ValueError('Remote main changed')
        subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(args.root), 'merge-base', '--is-ancestor',
                        remote['refs/heads/main'], args.carrier], check=True)
        selected = [f'refs/{prefix}/{p["namespace"]}/{suffix}'
                    for p in inputs['products']
                    for prefix, suffix in [('tags', 'v3.1.0-rc.1'), ('tags', 'v3.1.0')]]
        selected += [f'refs/heads/{kind}/{p["namespace"]}/3.1.0'
                     for p in inputs['products'] for kind in ['rc', 'release']]
        if any(ref in remote for ref in selected):
            raise ValueError('A selected new release ref already exists remotely')
        rows = [{'destination': 'refs/heads/main', 'source': args.carrier,
                 'object': args.carrier, 'commit': args.carrier, 'immutable': False,
                 'expected_remote': remote['refs/heads/main']}]
        products = []
        for product in inputs['products']:
            namespace = product['namespace']
            commit = args.native if product['commit_role'] == 'native-A' else args.carrier
            tree = git(args.root, 'rev-parse', commit + ':' + product['root'])
            products.append({**product, 'commit': commit, 'subtree_tree': tree})
            for tag in [f'{namespace}/v3.1.0-rc.1', f'{namespace}/v3.1.0']:
                ref = 'refs/tags/' + tag
                if ref != 'refs/tags/specification_methodology/v3.1.0-rc.1':
                    message = f'STDO 3.1.0-rc.1 {namespace}\n\nSTDO-Product-Subtree: {product["root"]}\nSTDO-Product-Tree: {tree}\nSTDO-Native-Commit: {args.native}\nSTDO-Cohort-Profile: rc-six-product-v1\n'
                    subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(args.root), 'tag', '-a', tag, commit,
                                    '-F', '-'], input=message, text=True, check=True)
                rows.append({'destination': ref, 'source': ref,
                             'object': git(args.root, 'rev-parse', ref), 'commit': commit,
                             'immutable': tag.endswith('-rc.1'), 'expected_remote': ''})
            for kind in ['rc', 'release']:
                ref = f'refs/heads/{kind}/{namespace}/3.1.0'
                subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(args.root), 'update-ref', ref, commit,
                                '0' * 40], check=True)
                rows.append({'destination': ref, 'source': ref, 'object': commit,
                             'commit': commit, 'immutable': False, 'expected_remote': ''})
        plan = {'kind': 'stdo.atomic-ref-transaction/1', 'version': '3.1.0-rc.1',
                'products': products, 'refs': rows, 'qualified_files': qualification['qualified_files'] +
                    [{'path': str(args.qualification.resolve()), 'sha256': hashlib.sha256(args.qualification.read_bytes()).hexdigest()}]}
        write(args.plan, plan)
    else:
        plan = json.loads(args.plan.read_text())
    check(args.root, plan, remote)
    result = {'mode': args.mode, 'local_graph_valid': True, 'remote_expectations_match': True,
              'plan_sha256': hashlib.sha256(args.plan.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), 'refs': len(plan['refs'])}
    if args.mode == 'publish':
        argv = ['git', '-C', str(args.root), 'push', '--atomic']
        argv += [f'--force-with-lease={r["destination"]}:{r["expected_remote"]}' for r in plan['refs']]
        argv += ['origin'] + [r['source'] + ':' + r['destination'] for r in plan['refs']]
        proc = subprocess.run(argv, capture_output=True, text=True)
        result.update({'argv': argv, 'exit': proc.returncode, 'stdout': proc.stdout, 'stderr': proc.stderr})
        write(args.result, result)
        if proc.returncode:
            raise SystemExit(proc.returncode)
        after = remote_refs(args.root)
        for row in plan['refs']:
            if after.get(row['destination']) != row['object']:
                raise ValueError('Remote readback mismatch: ' + row['destination'])
            if row['destination'].startswith('refs/tags/') and after.get(row['destination'] + '^{}') != row['commit']:
                raise ValueError('Remote peel mismatch: ' + row['destination'])
        result['remote_readback_valid'] = True
    write(args.result, result)
    print(json.dumps({key: value for key, value in result.items() if key not in ['argv', 'stdout', 'stderr']}))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
